> 2. Before every move, list every enemy capture and check whether my piece is defended. Check that the square I move to is not attacked and that nothing blocks my own lines (I once missed that a pawn blocked my rook).
> 3. Never leave a piece undefended where a pawn or piece can take it for free.
> 4. Before recapturing or trading, count the full exchange sequence.
> 5. Keep the king safe; watch diagonals toward g7/f7 and open files.
> 6. Avoid speculative sacrifices unless calculated to a clear win.
> 7. When ahead in material, trade pieces and keep the position simple.
> 8. Opponent A01 plays 1.e4, the Italian with c3/d4, and likes active pieces and early queen trades.
> 9. Keep thinking brief on routine moves.
